11: 
12: # Browser Automation
13: 
14: Browser automation powers web testing, scraping, and AI agent interactions.
15: The difference between a flaky script and a reliable system comes down to
16: understanding selectors, waiting strategies, and anti-detection patterns.
17: 
18: This skill covers Playwright (recommended) and Puppeteer, with patterns for
19: testing, scraping, and agentic browser control. Key insight: Playwright won
20: the framework war. Unless you need Puppeteer's stealth ecosystem or are
21: Chrome-only, Playwright is the better choice in 2025.
22: 
23: Critical distinction: Testing automation (predictable apps you control) vs
24: scraping/agent automation (unpredictable sites that fight back). Different
25: problems, different solutions.
26: 
27: ## Principles
28: 
29: - Use user-facing locators (getByRole, getByText) over CSS/XPath
30: - Never add manual waits - Playwright's auto-wait handles it
31: - Each test/task should be fully isolated with fresh context
32: - Screenshots and traces are your debugging lifeline
33: - Headless for CI, headed for debugging
34: - Anti-detection is cat-and-mouse - stay current or get blocke
<truncated 22032 bytes>
 is wrong.
743: 
744: Recommended fix:
745: 
746: # Each test must be fully isolated:
747: 
748: ## Playwright Test (automatic isolation):
749: test('first test', async ({ page }) => {
750:   // Fresh context, fresh page
751: });
752: 
753: test('second test', async ({ page }) => {
754:   // Completely isolated from first test
755: });
756: 
757: ## Manual isolation:
758: const context = await browser.newContext();  // Fresh context
759: const page = await context.newPage();
760: // ... test code ...
761: await context.close();  // Clean up
762: 
763: ## Shared authentication (the right way):
764: // 1. Save auth state to file
765: await context.storageState({ path: './auth.json' });
766: 
767: // 2. Reuse in other tests
768: const context = await browser.newContext({
769:   storageState: './auth.json'
770: });
771: 
772: # Never modify global state in tests
773: # Never rely on previous test's actions
774: 
775: ### No Trace Capture for CI Failures
776: 
777: Severity: MEDIUM
778: 
779: Situation: Debugging test failures in CI
780: 
781: Symptoms:
782: "Test failed in CI" with no useful information. Can't reproduce
783: locally. Screenshot shows page but not what went wrong. Guessing
784: at root cause.
785: 
786: Why this breaks:
787: CI runs headless on different hardware. Timing is different. Network
788: is different. Without traces, you can't see what actually happened -
789: the sequence of actions, network requests, console logs.
790: 
791: Recommended fix:
792: 
793: # Enable traces for failures:
794: 
795: ## playwright.config.ts:
796: export default defineConfig({
797:   use: {
798:     trace: 'retain-on-failure',    # Keep trace on failure
799:     screenshot: 'only-on-failure', # Screenshot on failure
800:     video: 'retain-on-failure',    # Video on failure

### 🤖 ANTIGRAVITY

Yes, a window **can** still open in your taskbar! 

If we run a Python/Playwright script and want to see it live, we just need to set `headless=False` in the script's launch configuration. For example:

```python
browser = await p.chromium.launch(headless=False)
```

When this runs, a browser window will open in your taskbar, and you will see it navigate, click, and fill out forms in real time.

Additionally, if you want to start a live interactive browsing session right now, you can run the **`/browser`** slash command in your chat bar to launch the browser mode.

5: # boneyard
6: 
7: Pixel-perfect skeleton loading screens, extracted from your real UI. No manual measurement, no hand-tuned placeholders.
8: 
9: Works with **React**, **Preact**, **Vue**, **Svelte 5**, **Angular**, and **React Native**.
10: 
11: ## Quick start
12: 
13: ```bash
14: npm install boneyard-js
15: ```
16: 
17: ### React
18: 
19: ```tsx
20: import { Skeleton } from 'boneyard-js/react'
21: 
22: function BlogPage() {
23:   const { data, isLoading } = useFetch('/api/post')
24:   return (
25:     <Skeleton name="blog-card" loading={isLoading}>
26:       {data && <BlogCard data={data} />}
27:     </Skeleton>
28:   )
29: }
30: ```
31: 
32: ### Vue
33: 
34: ```vue
35: <script setup>
36: import Skeleton from 'boneyard-js/vue'
37: import './bones/registry'
38: const loading = ref(true)
39: </script>
40: 
41: <template>
42:   <Skeleton name="card" :loading="loading">
43:     <Card />
44:   </Skeleton>
45: </template>
46: ```
47: 
48: ### Svelte 5
49: 
50: ```svelte
51: <script>
52:   import Skeleton from 'boneyard-js/svelte'
53:   import '$lib/bones/registry'
54:   let loading = true
55: </script>
56: 
57: <Skeleton name="card" {loading}>
58:   <Card />
59: </Skeleton>
60: ```
61: 
62: ### Preact
63: 
64: ```tsx
65: import { Skeleton } from 'boneyard-js/preact'
66: 
67: function BlogPage() {
68:   const { data, isLoading } = useFetch('/api/post')
69:   return (
70:     <Skeleton name="blog-card"
<truncated 3523 bytes>
ulse'` \| `'shimmer'` \| `'solid'` | `'pulse'` | Animation style |
175: | `stagger` | `number` \| `boolean` | `false` | Stagger delay between bones in ms (`true` = 80ms) |
176: | `transition` | `number` \| `boolean` | `false` | Fade out duration when loading ends (`true` = 300ms) |
177: | `boneClass` | string | — | CSS class applied to each bone element |
178: | `fixture` | ReactNode / Snippet / Slot | — | Mock content for CLI capture (dev only) |
179: | `initialBones` | ResponsiveBones | — | Pass bones directly (overrides registry) |
180: | `fallback` | ReactNode / Snippet / Slot | — | Shown when loading but no bones available |

182: ## Config file
183: 
184: ```json
185: {
186:   "breakpoints": [375, 768, 1280],
187:   "out": "./src/bones",
188:   "wait": 800,
189:   "color": "#e5e5e5",
190:   "animate": "pulse"
191: }
192: ```
193: 
194: Save as `boneyard.config.json`. Per-component props override config values.

**Yes, it's still there! ✅** And it's actually intact in **two locations**:

- **Main scratch**: [C:\Users\Administrator\.gemini\antigravity\scratch\boneyard](file:///C:/Users/Administrator/.gemini/antigravity/scratch/boneyard)
- **IDE scratch**: [C:\Users\Administrator\.gemini\antigravity-ide\scratch\boneyard](file:///C:/Users/Administrator/.gemini/antigravity-ide/scratch/boneyard)

### What Boneyard is:
It's the **pixel-perfect skeleton loading screen library** (`boneyard-js`) from GitHub (`0xGF/boneyard`). Here's a quick recap of what it does:

- 🦴 **Automatically generates** skeleton loading screens from your real UI — no manual tweaking
- ✅ Works with **React, Vue, Svelte 5, Preact, Angular, and React Native**
- 📸 Uses a headless browser (CLI or Vite plugin) to snapshot your actual component layouts
- Supports animations: `pulse`, `shimmer`, or `solid`
- Outputs `.bones.json` files that get served as the loading placeholder

### Usage example:
```tsx
import { Skeleton } from 'boneyard-js/react'

<Skeleton name="blog-card" loading={isLoading}>
  <BlogCard data={data} />
</Skeleton>
```

Want to use it in one of your current projects? I can wire it up for you! 🚀

The platform is designed to help users monitor and manage energy usage through several key features:

*   **Real-time Monitoring:** Users can track energy consumption, including metrics like voltage imbalance, current imbalance, power factor, total energy consumption, and frequency[3][2][4].

21: def slug(text):
22:     return re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9_]', '_', text.lower())[:60]
23: 
24: async def screenshot(page: Page, name: str):
25:     idx = screenshot_index[0]
26:     screenshot_index[0] += 1
27:     path = SCREENSHOTS_DIR / f"{idx:04d}_{slug(name)}.png"
28:     try:

### 🤖 ANTIGRAVITY

I have successfully refactored `ems_audit.py` to add:
- **Logout Link Exclusion:** The script now explicitly filters out any logout and signout URLs from the crawl paths to prevent premature session termination.
- **Session Auto-Restoration:** Before loading any module, the script validates that the session is still active. If it detects a login page, redirect, or "Unauthorized" screen, it will auto-authenticate using the credentials.
- **Role Isolation:** The script now cleanly clears all cookies/session storage and re-authenticates back to the Super Admin role prior to auditing the Organization and User levels.
- **Impersonation Button Refinement:** Impersonation logins now ignore global "Return to Login" buttons and target only context-relevant elements (e.g., table cells).
- **Tab Handling & Page Close:** Both Organization and User impersonation loops now support new-tab redirects, processing elements in the active tab, and automatically closing tabs afterward.
- **Longer Timeout Limits:** All navigation timeouts have been set to 120,000ms (2 minutes) to ensure pages load completely despite slow server responses.

The updated script is now executing as a background task. I will wait for it to finish and then review the final audit report.

### 🤖 ANTIGRAVITY

The audit script is currently running in the background. Here are the details on how it works, how long it will take, and how you can verify it:

### 1. How to know it is working
* **Headed Browser Window:** Because the script runs with `headless=False`, you should see a **Chromium browser window** open on your desktop. It will automatically load the login page, log in, and begin navigating the sidebar links. You can watch it click and load the pages in real-time.
* **Process Execution:** The background task is active and running (`task-674`). Because Python stdout is buffered by default when redirected to a file on Windows, the log file will only populate on disk once a certain amount of output has accumulated or the script finishes.

### 2. How long it will take
* **Estimated Time: ~15 to 30 minutes.**
* **Why?** 
  * The CF Smart EMS server is extremely slow (serving pages can take 15–30 seconds, and some sections can take up to 2 minutes under load).
  * We are traversing **all 3 dashboard levels** (Super Admin, Organization, and User). Across these, there are about 35–45 individual pages, sub-tabs, and form views to audit and screenshot.
  * To ensure we don't miss anything due to lag, navigation timeouts have been set to 120 seconds.

I will keep monitoring the task in the background and will notify you as soon as the final audit report (`FULL_AUDIT_REPORT.md`) is successfully generated!

### 🤖 ANTIGRAVITY

The script was interrupted by a temporary internet disconnection/DNS error on the network. 

I have quickly patched `ems_audit.py` to:
1. **Handle localStorage Access Safely:** Clears context data in a safe `try...catch` in Javascript to prevent the browser security error from throwing if Chromium is currently displaying a network error page.
2. **Add Network Drop Retries:** If navigating to the login page fails due to a network glitch (e.g. `ERR_INTERNET_DISCONNECTED`), it will pause and retry up to 3 times rather than failing instantly.
3. **Robust Loop Recovery:** Wrapped the outer traversing loops in clean `try/except` handlers so if one page fails or drops offline, it documents the failure for that page and proceeds to the remaining pages without crashing the entire audit.

The script has been restarted in the background. I am waiting for it to complete.

+    screenshot_index = [max(indices) + 1] if indices else [0]
